INTRODUCTION  
The Division of Capital Asset Management & Maintenance (DCAMM) is pleased t o publish the 
Commonwealth of Massachusetts Real Property Report for 2026.  The Report includes 
Commonwealth propert y, both owned and leased.  In addition t o this PDF version of the report, 
DCAMM als o maintains a fully searchable online version (https://www.mass.gov/info-
details/commonwealth-report- on-real-property ) of the report available on DCAMM ’s website.  This 
PDF version represents a current snapshot (as of February 2026 ) of the land and building 
information at the time of publication.  The online version, howeve r, is updated monthly.  It 
incorporates improved accessibility features, along with several othe r user-friendly enhancements 
such as cust om filtering and basic mapping of properties. 
This report has been prepared in accordance with the requirements of M.G. L Chapter 7C, Section 
39. The information contained herein was compiled using the Commonwealth Asset Management 
Information System (“CAMIS”), an enterprise-wide platform overseen by the DCAMM Office of 
Finance and Administration to tr ack the Commonwealth ’s land and building portfolio, as wel l as 
DCAMM’s centralized leasing database.  Please see the “Dat a Sources” section below fo r further 
background on the Real Property Report data.
REPORT ORGANIZ ATION 
This PDF version of the report contains five tables varying by content and views of properties 
occupied by Commonwealth agencies (including both owned and leased) :  
•Table 1 - Summary of Commonwealth -Owned Real Property by Executive Office and Agency
•Table 2 - Summary of Commonwealth -Owned Real Property by Executive Office, Agency, and Municipality
•Table 3 - Summary of Commonwealth -Leased Real Property by Executive Office, Agency, and Municipality 
•Table 4 - Summary of County -Owned Real Property by User Agency, County, and Municipality
•Table 5 - Summary of Public/Private -Owned, State Restricted Real Property
Each table provides a range of specific details depending on context .  Tables 1 and 2 summarize the 
number and square footage of buildings categorized as either major or minor buildings  for each 
location.  Please refer to the online version  for detailed listing of all buildings in the inventory, 
include non -building improvements at each site.  
MORE ABOUT WHAT'S INCLUDED IN THE REAL PROPERTY REPORT  
Leases of Private Space for State Agencies  
The online report includes a separate page dedicated to leases of privately -owned space procured 
through DCAMM to house operations of a wide variety of state agencies.   This section of the report 
includes the name of the leasing agency, leased address, lea sed area (useable square feet), and 
key dates. The online report allows the user to filter by many of these attributes.   The leases may 
2

include parking spots and other ancillary spaces provided by the landlord.   Also included in our 
lease reporting are the limited  number of third -party licenses.  
Some state entities, including the University of Massachusetts, procure leases on their own 
authority, outside of DCAMM.   Those leases are not included here.   Some security sensitive leased 
locations (e.g., law enforcement), and leases of group homes opera ted by Executive Office of 
Health and Human Service agencies are not considered a public record and are also excluded from 
this online lease reporting.   
Data on Authority -owned Property  
For higher education campuses, Tables 1 and 2 of this PDF version include information available for 
land and buildings owned by Massachusetts State College Building Authority ( MSCBA), UMass 
Building Authority (UMBA), along with campus – specific Higher Education Assistance Corps. and 
foundations. These buildings owned by these entities contribute significantly to the mission of each 
campus. However, each respective entity should be contacted directly for the definitive current 
inventory.  
Assets owned by other authorities and independent agencies are included in this report only if the 
Commonwealth uses or occupies those facilities.  Assets owned by authorities such as MWRA, MBTA, 
and Massport and other independent agencies are not included  in this report except in rare cases 
when assets are used or occupied by the Commonwealth.  These entities should be contacted 
directly for information about their holdings.    
Conservation, Agricultural Restrictions and Certain Easements  
The online report also lists property with conservation restrictions and agricultural preservation 
restrictions plus certain easements.   Legal ownership of these lands remains with a municipal or 
private party.   The Commonwealth only owns restrictions on c ertain “development rights” tied to 
the property, not the property itself, but reflect the Commonwealth’s commitment to preserving 
open space.  
County-owned Land Housing Sheriffs and Court Facilities  
The report also includes county -owned land and buildings that are occupied by various Sheriff’s 
and Trial Court departments.   These buildings and associated land are not included in any other 
parts of the online report.  
REPORT HISTORY AND DATA SOURCES  
In the late 1980's, the Massachusetts Asset Management System (MAssets) staff collected data 
from all 351 municipalities across the Commonwealth and from state agencies reporting their land 
and building holdings. In 1999 pursuant to legislative authorizati on, DCAMM initiated the Capital 
Asset Management Information System (CAMIS). Through this program DCAMM conducted a 
comprehensive condition assessment of the state’s capital assets and procured software to 
manage the collected data. In 2015 the MAssets dat a was merged into the CAMIS database.  
3

Now, through its office of Finance and Administration, DCAMM has implemented the CAMIS 
program to both assist facilities in their daily maintenance activities and to provide data for DCAMM 
staff to optimize statewide capital maintenance and repair planning , and to track state land and 
building assets. CAMIS staff consult with facilities' staff throughout the state to update information 
about their buildings and improvements. DCAMM also consults with assessors of cities and towns 
statewide to verify informat ion about parcels in their municipalities, and with various state 
agencies to confirm their land holdings and update any change in status.  
DCAMM’s Office of Real Estate Management (OREM) manages the purchase and sale of real 
property for most Commonwealth agencies. OREM works with the CAMIS team and DCAMM’s 
Office of the General Counsel to ensure that each acquisition and disposition of land and buildings 
is logged into the Commonwealth portfolio of real property. They also report on telecom leases on 
state property, easements, land swaps, and care & control agreements between agencies.  
The Office of Leasing  (within OREM ) maintains information on properties leased on behalf of 
Commonwealth agencies. The information contained in the Leasing database is constantly 
changing, based on the commencement and expiration, as well as the terms of each agreement. 
The Leasing Office d iligently seeks the most appropriate and inexpensive rental space available to 
suit each agency’s needs and records information on each agreement in their database as each 
transaction is completed.  
The information in the Real Property database is dynamic. This RPR publication is therefore a 
comprehensive annual snapshot of the existing portfolio. Due to the tremendous volume of land 
and building data gathered over decades, DCAMM and its agency partner s are constantly reviewing 
CAMIS data to ensure accuracy and making changes to the data when warranted.  
 
ONGOING DATA QUALITY IMPROVEMENTS  
The annual Real Property Report presents a snapshot of a dynamic land and building portfolio.   
DCAMM continues with systematic quality review and technology enhancements for CAMIS 
reporting, including implementing an online searchable version of the repor t where the public can 
access information updated monthly (see online version ). 
For comments or questions about this report or any Commonwealth -owned property, please contact 
the Division of Capital Asset Management & Maintenance, Office of Finance and Administration, One 
Ashburton Place, Boston, MA 02108 or at info.DCAMM@mass.gov .

Table 3 
Summary of Commonwealth- Leased Real Property by Executive O Ưice, 
Agency, and Municipality  
Includes aggregated summary by Secretariat along with more detailed
